- **Konnor Griffin was activated from the 60-day IL on Sept. 4** after the left ring-finger sagittal-band injury and immediately returned to the lineup.
- **Ryan O'Hearn remains out** with a left-quad strain and is not expected back until later September.
- **Kirby Yates is on the 15-day IL** with right-shoulder impingement.
- Endy Rodríguez is out after left-hip surgery.
- Angels LHP Yusei Kikuchi was activated from the 60-day IL on Aug. 23 following left-shoulder inflammation.
- Wade Meckler returned from the concussion IL on Aug. 28 and is in the lineup.
- Nolan Schanuel had been placed on the IL with a right intercostal strain and is not in the current lineup.
- Gustavo Campero remains out with a right-hamstring strain.

## Starter regime

### Braxton Ashcraft
2026 season:
- 14-5, 3.59 ERA.
- 160.1 IP.
- 164 SO.
- 1.079 WHIP.

Last five starts:
- 5.0 IP, 2 ER at STL.
- 6.0 IP, 1 ER at SD.
- 8.0 IP, 1 ER vs DET.
- 9.0 IP, 1 ER at MIA.
- 5.0 IP, 3 ER at MIL.

August:
- **2.13 ERA across six starts**, 29 strikeouts and only five walks in 38 innings.

Interpretation:
- Strong, stable current starter centre.
- Meaningful 6+ inning branch.
- Not merely ERA-driven: recent walk suppression and innings depth support the run-suppression process.
- The Angels lineup has top-end quality through Neto/Trout/Meckler but is thin in the lower half.

### Yusei Kikuchi
2026 season line is distorted by injury interruption:
- 0-5, 5.65 ERA in 43 innings.
- Missed roughly four months with left-shoulder inflammation.
- Activated Aug. 23.

Two starts after returning:
- at Texas: 5.0 IP, 5 ER.
- vs Philadelphia: **7.0 IP, 2 ER**, 6 H, 1 BB, 6 K.

Interpretation:
- The 5.65 ERA should not be used as a stable current run-rate estimate.
- Return-from-injury uncertainty remains wide.
- The 7-inning Philadelphia start is genuine evidence that his pre-injury/quality-start ceiling has returned, but two starts cannot collapse the wide distribution.
- Pittsburgh's matchup is favorable to Kikuchi relative to average because the Pirates have struggled badly against left-handed pitching.

## Team / platoon context

Baseball-Reference pregame splits:
- Angels: **40-58 vs RHP**, 53-88 overall, **1-9 in last 10**.
- Pirates: 70-72 overall, **6-4 in last 10**, but only **14-30 vs LHP**.
- Pirates are 36-33 at home.
- Angels are 23-43 away.

Recent offence:
- Angels scored **3.15 runs/game over the previous 13 games**, batting roughly .200/.276/.305 (.582 OPS) over that span.
- Pittsburgh scored **39 runs in its last 10 games (3.9/game)**, but that includes a 13-run outlier; the recent run distribution contains several 0-3 run games.
- Friday's series opener finished 1-0 Pittsburgh, but that result is used only as context for bullpen usage and current participants, never as “momentum” or an automatic continuation signal.

Current Pirates lineup handedness is important:
- Horwitz L, Lowe L, Cruz L, Jacob Gonzalez L.
- Reynolds and Mangum are switch hitters.
This increases Kikuchi's plausible suppression branch and prevents treating Pittsburgh's better team record as a high-run assumption.

## Bullpen / previous-game workload

Friday:
- Jared Jones threw six shutout innings for Pittsburgh.
- Wilber Dotel and Mason Montgomery finished the shutout; Montgomery earned save No. 9.
- This means two meaningful Pirates relief arms worked Friday and are not treated as perfectly fresh.
- Ryan Johnson gave the Angels six innings of one-run ball, so the Angels bullpen was required for only the final three innings.

Pirates bullpen availability is also reduced by Kirby Yates' IL placement.
The relief-quality advantage still leans Pittsburgh overall, but not by enough to erase late-run variance.

## Weather / park
Final pregame warmup conditions:
- about **80°F**.
- cloudy.
- wind around **7 mph out to center field**.

Mechanism:
- modestly increases the fly-ball/HR upper tail.
- does not dominate the starter and lineup suppression evidence.
- rain/termination risk did not surface as a material pregame issue.

## Joint score tree

Central qualitative corridor:
- Angels: **1–3 runs**
- Pirates: **2–4 runs**

Representative states:
- PIT 3-2 → Under 8.5, Pirates ML, Angels +1.5, Under 6.5.
- PIT 4-2 → Under 8.5, Pirates ML, Under 6.5; Angels +1.5 loses.
- PIT 4-3 → Under 8.5, Pirates ML, Angels +1.5, Over 6.5.
- LAA 3-2 → Under 8.5, Angels +1.5, Under 6.5; Pirates ML loses.
- PIT 5-3 → Under 8.5, Pirates ML, Over 6.5.
- PIT 5-4 → Over 8.5, Pirates ML, Angels +1.5.
- 3-3 after nine → extra-inning branch materially increases the chance of finishing above 6.5.

Most coherent central family: **Pirates 3-2**, with 4-2 and 4-3 the nearest alternatives.

## Frozen top-four ranking

### 1. Under 8.5 runs — LEAN / MEDIUM
Why:
- Ashcraft has the best and most stable current starter profile in the game.
- Angels offence is in a weak current regime: 3.15 R/G over 13 games and .582 OPS.
- Kikuchi's season ERA overstates his current uncertainty because it spans the pre-injury and injury-interrupted regime; his latest start was seven innings, two runs.
- Pirates are only 14-30 versus left-handed pitching.
- Both lineups have meaningful holes in the lower half.

Kill paths:
- Kikuchi's shoulder-return variance produces a short 4-5 run start.
- wind-out conditions turn one fly-ball inning into a multi-run HR cluster.
- bullpen transitions or automatic-runner extras produce a late scoring spike.

### 2. Pittsburgh Pirates ML — LEAN / MEDIUM
Why:
- Ashcraft starter edge.
- Pirates 70-72 / Angels 53-88 overall.
- Pirates 6-4 last 10 vs Angels 1-9.
- Pittsburgh home field.
- Griffin's return improves speed/defence and lengthens the lineup.

Why not Rank 1:
- Pittsburgh's 14-30 record vs LHP is a genuine matchup drag.
- Kikuchi just demonstrated a 7-IP/2-ER quality branch.
- O'Hearn is out and Pittsburgh's lineup contains multiple left-handed bats against Kikuchi.

### 3. Angels +1.5 — LEAN / MEDIUM-LOW
This covers every Angels win and every one-run Angels loss.

Support:
- Low central score distribution creates a large one-run family.
- Kikuchi's restored quality-start branch makes 2-1, 3-2 and 4-3 Pittsburgh outcomes plausible.
- Pittsburgh's LHP struggles reduce separation probability.
- Ashcraft can dominate without Pittsburgh necessarily scoring enough to win by 2+.

Kill paths:
- Ashcraft throws 6-8 low-run innings while Kikuchi allows 3-4.
- Pittsburgh's speed and contact manufacture an extra run.
- Angels' lower-order offence contributes little and Pirates win 4-1 / 4-2.

### 4. Under 6.5 runs — FORCED RANK / MEDIUM-LOW
Why:
- The 3-2 and 4-2 branches are coherent.
- Ashcraft's current suppression and Angels' recent offence support 1-2 LAA runs.
- Pittsburgh's LHP split supports only 2-4 PIT runs despite the team-form gap.

Why only fourth:
- 6.5 is a very low MLB threshold.
- 4-3 loses immediately despite fitting the side analysis.
- warm wind-out conditions preserve HR-cluster risk.
- extra innings are a higher run-rate state under MLB's automatic-runner rule.
- Pirates used Dotel/Montgomery Friday and the bullpen is missing Yates.

## Unselected lines

### Over 6.5 — next / Rank 5
A real branch and closer to the centre than Over 8.5. It wins on 4-3, 5-2, 5-3 and most extra-inning results. It is omitted because the combined starter/matchup evidence leaves 3-2 / 4-2 slightly more central.

### Over 8.5 — Rank 6
Requires 9+ runs and depends on a starter collapse, HR cluster, bullpen failure or extended extras. It is the least likely of the six supplied endpoints.

## Potential winner
**Pittsburgh Pirates — qualitative winner lean.**

The winning mechanism is primarily Ashcraft's current starter advantage plus stronger overall roster/form and home field. It is not a wide-margin thesis; the central family is Pittsburgh by one run.

## 2026 diagnostic scoring
West Coast:
- R1 lost GWS 42-54.
- R2 lost Gold Coast 34-69.
- R3 lost Adelaide 43-60.
Average for 39.7, against 61.0; losing margins 12,35,17.

Melbourne:
- R1 beat Hawthorn 60-41.
- R2 beat GWS 60-12.
- R3 beat Brisbane 89-33.
Average for 69.7, against 28.7; winning margins 19,48,56.

Raw points/margins are diagnostic only under RULES_AFL.

Context:
- Melbourne's 56-point Round 3 win is shrunk because Brisbane was severely depleted in-game and had only two fit players on the bench for much of the match.
- West Coast generated the first 10 inside-50s of the final quarter against Adelaide but converted that territorial run into only one goal.
- West Coast also had 15 more inside-50s than GWS in Round 1 but lost, reinforcing the distinction between territory and conversion.
- Melbourne defender Tahlia Gillard had 11 intercepts and 10 marks against Brisbane, a strong suppression mechanism against West Coast's imperfect conversion.

## H2H
Round 6, 2025: Melbourne 10.14 (74) defeated West Coast 3.5 (23), margin 51.
Melbourne controlled stoppages and turnovers. Kept as continuity context only, not a direct 2026 forecast.

## Venue / weather
Mineral Resources Park: approximately 165 x 130 m, outdoor/exposed.
BOM Lathlain forecast for Sunday: cloudy, possible shower, maximum around 20-21C, southeasterly winds about 20-30 km/h.

AF-P4 limitation:

- Therefore wind is not assigned a deterministic Over/Under sign.

## Joint score tree
Qualitative corridor:
- Melbourne 56-64
- West Coast 30-38

Representative states:

- Melbourne 61-34 = 95: West Coast +33.5 / Over 94.5.

- Melbourne 70-32 = 102: Melbourne -33.5 / Over 94.5.

Central family: roughly Melbourne 60-34.

## Why the ranking
### 1. West Coast +33.5
- West Coast has lost by more than 33.5 only once in three 2026 matches, by 35.
- Other losses were 12 and 17.
- Roux, Dalgleish and Russo improve the home side's forward resources.
- Melbourne loses Austin and Pearce.
- Melbourne's 56-point Round 3 margin is shrunk for Brisbane's injury depletion.
- Melbourne travels west; West Coast is at home.

Kill path: Melbourne's midfield/intercept pressure traps West Coast, the Eagles again fail to convert entries, and the Dees create late separation.

### 2. Under 94.5
Support:
- Austin/Pearce absences reduce Melbourne's scoring/territory ceiling.
- Melbourne has conceded only 28.7 per game through three rounds.
- West Coast's recurring issue is conversion rather than entry generation.
- Wind broadens the low-conversion branch.

Why only medium-low:
- 94.5 sits near the central corridor.
- West Coast's three matches have all totalled 96+.
- A 60-35 result already beats the Over.

### 3. Over 94.5
Support:
- West Coast has allowed 54,69,60.
- Melbourne has scored 60,60,89.
- West Coast's returning forwards can add enough to turn a 60-65 point Melbourne state into 95+.
It ranks below Under because of Melbourne's two material outs and West Coast's conversion problem.

### 4. Melbourne -33.5
Support:
- Melbourne won its last two by 48 and 56.
- Last H2H margin was 51.
- Melbourne's midfield/intercept structure is much stronger.

Why fourth:
- The line demands separation, not merely a Melbourne win.
- West Coast's home venue and three attacking returns improve margin resistance.
- Melbourne's two outs weaken the cleanest 34+ point branches.
- A comfortable 60-34 Melbourne win still loses -33.5.

## Potential winner
**Melbourne / Narrm.**
Melbourne is 3-0, has the stronger contest/defensive structure and a materially higher scoring-shot ceiling. West Coast is 0-3 and has repeatedly failed to convert territory cleanly.

The winner thesis is separate from -33.5. Central margin is roughly 22-32 points.

## Revised joint score tree

Central qualitative corridor:
- Doosan: **3-4 runs**, with a 5-run rebound branch.
- SSG: **2-4 runs**.

Representative states:
- DOO 4-3 → U10.5 + SSG +1.5; DOO -1.5 loses; O8.5 loses.
- DOO 5-3 → U10.5 + DOO -1.5; SSG +1.5 loses; O8.5 loses.
- SSG 4-3 → U10.5 + SSG +1.5; DOO -1.5 loses; O8.5 loses.
- DOO 5-4 → U10.5 + SSG +1.5 + O8.5; DOO -1.5 loses.
- DOO 6-3 → U10.5 + DOO -1.5 + O8.5.
- SSG 6-4 → U10.5 + SSG +1.5 + O8.5.
- 6-5 either way → O8.5 and loses U10.5.

Most coherent family after the Yang Eui-ji late out:
**Doosan 4-3 / SSG 4-3 / Doosan 5-3**, with the Doosan winner sign only narrowly preferred.

## Rank 1 — Under 10.5
Why:
- Logue is the strongest starter and has suppressed SSG in both 2026 meetings.
- Doosan's offence has scored only four total runs over its previous five games.
- Yang Eui-ji is now out of the starting lineup.
- SSG has scored only 4 and 3 in the first two games of this series.
- Bullpens are not broadly exhausted.
- 4-3, 5-3, 5-4, 6-3 and 6-4 all remain Under 10.5.

Kill paths:
- Choi's control issues trigger an early hook and Doosan finally clusters runs.
- SSG's current HR form continues against Logue.
- Munhak produces multiple HR clusters.
- extra innings or bullpen failure pushes 11+.

## Rank 2 — SSG +1.5
Why:
- Captures every SSG win and every one-run SSG loss.
- Doosan's offensive separation mechanism is weaker without Yang Eui-ji.
- SSG has won both series games and Doosan has lost five straight.
- Logue can pitch well while Doosan wins only 3-2 or 4-3.
- The likely low-to-moderate total creates a large one-run game family, but the low total is not treated as proof the cushion is safe.

Kill paths:
- Logue dominates SSG and Choi's walks convert, creating 5-2 / 6-2.
- Severino/Yang Seok-hwan provide enough middle-order power despite Yang Eui-ji's absence.

## Rank 3 — Doosan -1.5
Why it remains above Over 8.5:
- Doosan still owns the strongest starting-pitcher edge.
- Choi's 5.79 ERA vs Doosan and his recent walk/HBP traffic provide a direct separation mechanism.
- A 5-3 or 6-3 Doosan win can cover while still remaining Under 10.5.

Why it is only third:
- Yang Eui-ji is out.
- Doosan has scored 0-2 runs repeatedly during the slump.
- -1.5 requires separation, not merely a Logue-backed win.
- 4-3 Doosan is central and loses the run line.

## Rank 4 — Over 8.5
Why it remains live:
- Choi's traffic/early-hook branch.
- Munhak's HR-sensitive environment.
- Jeon Ui-san has homered in three straight/current recent games across the series window.
- 5-4, 6-3, 6-4 all clear 8.5.

Why last:
- Yang Eui-ji's absence lowers Doosan's ceiling.
- Logue suppresses the SSG side of the total.
- Neither bullpen is broadly depleted.
- 4-3 and 5-3 are more coherent than 5-4/6-4.

## Potential winner
**Doosan Bears — narrow qualitative lean.**

The starter matchup remains enough to keep Doosan slightly ahead:
- Logue 0.79 ERA vs SSG in two 2026 starts.
- Choi 5.79 ERA vs Doosan.
- Doosan owns the stronger season record.

But Yang Eui-ji's late absence materially narrows the edge. The winner call is closer to **Doosan 4-3** than a multi-run favourite result.

The [completed Tennis.com match record](https://www.tennis.com/tournaments/us-open/matches/t-fritz-vs-f-cerundolo-2026-09-05) was freshly opened; the official US Open article was discoverable but its full-page fetch failed, so this is not described as a freshly read official report.

**Driver:** Fritz's two-set lead became a five-set defeat. The aggregate-game cushion and length predictions survived while the winner failed. The match record reports six Cerúndolo breaks against five for Fritz, consistent with return pressure on both sides. **Knowable:** the card favored Fritz in four sets but retained Cerúndolo's forehand/break-creation and a longer-match/upset route. It compared Fritz's short prior round with Cerúndolo's longer one and explicitly refused to treat workload as automatically negative. The comeback and any physical cause were not knowable.

**Smallest improvement:** keep legal score paths, game margin and winner probabilities separate. Do not infer that prior workload caused either the loss or resilience, and do not generalize a winning cushion to every match-winner score path. The successful top two are outcome evidence, not calibration of the unissued probabilities.

Wofford gained 208 yards to Gardner-Webb's 438; a 97-yard kickoff return touchdown, field position and a late goal-line interception mattered. The report records a 98-minute halftime storm delay.

**Knowable:** the frozen card's Wofford 27–34/Gardner-Webb 14–21 budget missed both outputs. It already distinguished Wofford's prior non-offensive scoring and noted Gardner-Webb's passing production and Wofford's allowed yardage. Heat and thunderstorm risk were mentioned; the exact delay, return and interception were not knowable.

**Smallest improvement:** separate offensive production, non-offensive scores and finishing/field position in the existing drive budget. The Under win does not validate an “ineffective Gardner-Webb offence” account in the face of 438 yards, or the team's predicted points. No weather coefficient is inferred from a single disrupted game.

The match stats show Darderi converting six of eight break chances and Sweeny two of eight; Darderi won 64% of second-serve points. **Knowable:** the card emphasized Darderi's vulnerable previous-round second serve and Sweeny's Musetti upset, but also retained opponent-level/regression concerns and an explicit efficient three-set Darderi win. Its competitive-four-set central branch was not realized. The exact serve recovery was unknown; a single opponent's return performance was never a permanent serve-quality measurement.

**Smallest improvement:** compare current serve/return evidence with opponent quality, and print the joint total/margin consequence of the existing three-set branch. This is a double miss with a correct winner; neither the winner nor naming the losing branch excuses the missed distribution. No automatic fade of an upset winner is adopted.

Exact Barbados completion overs are not needed for these TKR targets and are not asserted from conflicting secondary accounts.

A new opening combination made those four historical Unders a weak ceiling argument. Final XI/toss were not verified at the pre-toss freeze; the exact explosive partnership and no-ball reprieve were unknown.

**Smallest improvement:** map historical phase evidence to today's likely batting roles before using it as a ceiling. Keep the unconfirmed-XI mixture explicit and distinguish early scoring from later wicket loss. This reinforces the existing roster/phase controls; it does not fit an automatic new-player scoring uplift or rewrite the pre-toss rank after the toss.

Preserve **start-crossed original-line view** status.

**Driver:** Valdez's run prevention and Detroit's damage against otherwise strong pitching defeated the Cleveland winner case. **Knowable:** the card stressed Valdez's recent walks/earned runs, Messick's form and Cleveland's lineup/bullpen issues, while retaining Valdez's groundball/deeper-start possibility. The Detroit 2–3/Cleveland 3–4 central allocation missed both teams. Exact homers and seven scoreless innings were unknown.

**Smallest improvement:** carry both starters' supported upside/downside and each lineup's scoring floor separately. Strikeouts and recent ERA do not guarantee the distribution of runs in one game. Cincinnati 5–3, total eight. **Ranks: Brewers -1.5 L; Over 9 L; Reds +1.5 W; Under 9 W.** Milwaukee winner L. Fresh [MLB feed 824468](https://statsapi.mlb.com/api/v1.1/game/824468/feed/live) records May's 3⅔ innings/two runs, Abbott's four innings/two runs and Cincinnati relief allowing one run over five innings. Both starters left early, yet the game stayed below nine.

**Knowable:** the card identified May/Abbott's recent struggles and hook risk, leaned on Milwaukee's record against left-handed starters and a season bullpen comparison, and left Milwaukee's official order unresolved. It also named relevant Cincinnati relief quality. The exact bullpen performance and late home runs were unknown. A team's record against left-handed starters is a team outcome statistic, not an individual batter split or proof against every subsequent reliever.

**Smallest improvement:** follow the named starter-to-reliever sequence and likely batting exposure rather than giving every early hook an Over sign. Retain lineup uncertainty in the final reasoning and reconcile Cincinnati's viable relief branch with the margin/total claim. The double miss supports scrutiny of the score-state chain; it does not validate a predetermined Reds reranking or a blanket Under rule.

### P-317 — Doosan Bears at SSG Landers, September 6

Doosan 16–9, total 25, margin seven. **Ranks: Under 10.5 L; SSG +1.5 L; Doosan -1.5 W; Over 8.5 W.** Doosan winner W. A freshly retrieved [Xports News venue report by Kim Geun-han](https://www.xportsnews.com/article/2192884) provides the final and scoring sequence. The official KBO route returned an unpopulated body rather than a usable box score. The earlier Ilgan Sports corroboration remains in the historical record, but its full-page re-fetch failed; no new claim of two fully retrieved independent sources is made.

**Source correction:** a Nate candidate surfaced as `[AI상보]` (AI report) and is excluded. Nate is an aggregator hostname, not proof of independent reporting. The earlier bare “isplus and nate, two independent reports” assertion is withdrawn as an unverified lineage claim; it does not by itself overturn a final independently documented by the named human venue reporter. The KBO result remains a reported research final with an official-box-score access limitation.

**Driver:** Logue actually supplied six innings of one-run pitching; the Under failed despite that successful starter premise. Doosan's scoring accelerated after Choi's four innings/three runs, including a seven-run sixth. SSG then scored eight in the eighth. This separates early starter quality, opponent relief failure and late score-state exposure.

**Knowable:** the frozen revision knew Yang Eui-ji was out, Doosan had scored four runs across five recent games, Choi had traffic/hook concerns and Logue had the stronger starting profile. It retained a Choi/relief failure branch. The exact hit/walk cluster, seven-run sixth and eight-run eighth were unknown. The central Doosan 3–4/SSG 2–4 allocation missed both outputs. **Smallest improvement:** keep the current lineup adjustment, but do not let a scoring drought or one strong starter suppress the other side's relief exposure and low-leverage late scoring. No automatic slump-rebound, scratch reversal or KBO Over rule is adopted.

---

# GENERAL LEARNINGS / SOURCES FOR THE P-288–P-317 MINI-LOG RETROSPECTIVE COHORT

## General learnings, rule changes, observations and sources

**Administrative and validity corrections applied now:** scan frozen slates before declaring archival omission; scan unresolved sub-rows even when the event says settled; count research gaps separately from operator questions; preserve explicit start-crossed labels; check source lineage rather than counting hostnames; describe HTTP errors as scoped access evidence. These implement existing METHOD/CONTROLS/G34/G36 obligations. No numerical weights, probabilities, automatic rank changes or new forecast gates were added.

**Repeated sporting observations:** strong early cricket scoring does not ensure a full-innings Over (P-305), and a slow start does not ensure an Under (P-217). Historical batting roles can change materially (P-311). Winner, margin and total allocation need separate assessments (P-309, P-312–315). Starter success can coexist with an extreme final total through later exposure (P-317). Possession, goals and corners are different processes (P-304/P-306/P-290). Winning wide cushions or totals can coexist with inaccurate score corridors (P-303/P-314). Legal tennis game sums remain necessary (P-291/P-308/P-310). These reinforce existing controls; the amount and direction of any future adjustment require a proper later-case test.

**Knowability discipline:** exact penalties, dismissals, injuries, no-ball reprieves, return touchdowns, homers and late collapses were post-issue observations. A forecast can be criticized for omitting an already-supported branch or mishandling an arithmetic definition; it cannot be charged with failing to know an exact random play. Conversely, listing every branch is not enough to demonstrate well-calibrated probabilities that were never issued. Current requirements are not silently attributed to historical versions.

| Source / newly demonstrated use | Fields supported in this pass | Limit / disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Official Chance Liga match detail, event 8382 | P-304 final/half, five Slavia corners, XI/bench/substitutions | NEW VERIFIED FIELD COVERAGE; league page identifies Stats Perform lineage; informational settlement use, no feature approval |
| ESPN soccer `mex.1`, event 401876990 | P-290 final and 1–3 corners | NEW VERIFIED EVENT/FIELD; one vendor lineage, operator wording still unknown |
| ESPN cricket event 1547886 | P-305 toss, PP 60/1, completed totals | Reverified; ESPNcricinfo is related, not an independent corroborator |
| ESPN cricket event 1534200 | P-217 rain/revised format, innings, 4.5-over mandatory PP | Reverified; mandatory PP is not six completed overs; do not relabel the field |
| Official CPL newsroom | P-311 batting-first condition, PP and result; P-217 late scoring | Official reporting supplements scorecard fields; do not infer missing overs or every delivery |
| MLB StatsAPI, games 824389 / 824468 / 823335 | P-312–314 final, innings and pitching/scoring sequence | Reverified official structured evidence; raw responses saved |
| Wofford official report and ESPN FCS | P-309 score, yards, return/interception, delay | Report establishes sequence, not a causal weather coefficient |
| AFL official report | P-315 final and quarter-specific territory reversal | Direct fetch succeeded where web open failed; no verified wind-to-end field |

| Xports News, named venue reporter | P-317 final and inning sequence | Fresh full report; official KBO box inaccessible; other re-fetches limited |
| Nate AI-labelled candidate | No accepted fields | EXCLUDED under existing synthetic-content rule; aggregator identity is not independence |

**Remaining research is specific:** obtain eight owner-confirmed corner fields and resolve P-126's fixture identity/state. Operator wording is separately missing for the four known special conditions. Repeating arbitrary ESPN league slugs or failed URLs is not a substitute for those fields, but a failed route is not a permanent ban on future research. No new source has been approved for numerical model features by this retrospective.
